\{\bar f_\epsilon(b_x+r_x\odot\cos(\vartheta-\tau))-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)\}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}J_K(\tau_\jmath)\,d\tau
+
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x) \\
&\qquad=
\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{\mathcal J}}
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x+r_x\odot\cos(\vartheta-\tau))
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}J_K(\tau_\jmath)\,d\tau
-
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{\mathcal J}}\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}J_K(\tau_\jmath)\,d\tau
+
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x) \\
&\qquad=
\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{\mathcal J}}
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x+r_x\odot\cos(\vartheta-\tau))
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}J_K(\tau_\jmath)\,d\tau .
\end{aligned}
\]
Thus
\[
P_{x,K}(b_x+r_x\odot\cos\vartheta)
=
\int_{[-\pi,\pi]^{\mathcal J}}
\bar f_\epsilon(b_x+r_x\odot\cos(\vartheta-\tau))
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}J_K(\tau_\jmath)\,d\tau.
\]
For each coordinate, \(J_K(-t)=J_K(t)\): both sine factors in its defining ratio change sign, and the ratio is raised to the fourth power. The coordinatewise substitution \(t=-\tau\) preserves \([-\pi,\pi]^{\mathcal J}\) and Lebesgue measure, and therefore changes \(\cos(\vartheta-\tau)\) to \(\cos(\vartheta+t)\) while leaving the product kernel unchanged. The last display is consequently the order-\(K\) tensor Jackson convolution in \cref{obj:def:jackson-kernel} of \(\bar f_\epsilon\) precomposed with the affine parametrization of \(Q_x\), evaluated at \(\vartheta\).
% lean: tensorJackson_integral_eq_one

\item Substituting the definition of \(S_x\) into the coefficient estimate gives
\[
\sum_{\beta\in\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal R_{x,K})}
|(\mathcal R_{x,K})_\beta|
\le
2^{40K+20}
(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
The constructed \(P_{x,K}\) and \(\mathcal R_{x,K}\) have the asserted physical degree, normalized degree, centered-evaluation identity, Jackson-convolution identity, and coefficient envelope.
% lean: jacksonTensorPolynomialData_exists
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The extraction step supplies the polynomial used by the estimator, and its centered form is the one matched to the factorial lift below. The dependence on \(\sum_{\jmath}r_{\jmath,x}\) is the local scale that later becomes the Poisson noise scale after the pilot rectangle is chosen from the data.

For reference, we also isolate the tensor convolution operator used in these statements. This notation matches the product-kernel approximation scheme standard in Jackson-type polynomial approximation \citep{DeVore1993}.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_20}[Tensor Jackson convolution operator $\mathcal J_K$]
For a positive integer $K$, let $J_K(t)=c_K\{\sin(Kt/2)/\sin(t/2)\}^4$ on $[-\pi,\pi]$, with the removable values filled in continuously and with $c_K$ chosen so that $\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}J_K(t)\,dt=1$. For a finite coordinate dimension $D$ and a function $f:\mathbb R^D\to\mathbb R$, the order-$K$ tensor Jackson convolution is the operator $\mathcal J_K$ defined, for $\theta\in\mathbb R^D$, by \[ (\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\theta) := \int_{[-\pi,\pi]^D} f\{\cos(\theta+t)\}\prod_{i=1}^D J_K(t_i)\,dt, \] where cosine and addition are coordinatewise. Equivalently, the product kernel $\prod_{i=1}^D J_K(t_i)$ is normalized to have integral one over $[-\pi,\pi]^D$.
\end{definitionv}

The approximation error is controlled through a weighted modulus along angular perturbations. The square-root boundary term in the main certificate comes from converting angular increments back to physical coordinates on a rectangle.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:jackson-convolution-modulus}[Tensor Jackson modulus]
Let \(D\) be a finite coordinate dimension, let \(K\) be a positive integer, and let \(f:\mathbb R^D\to\mathbb R\). Let \(L\ge0\), let \(a_i\ge0\) and \(b_i\ge0\) for each \(i\in[D]\), and fix an angle vector \(\varphi\in\mathbb R^D\). Suppose:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Kernel.)} The order-\(K\) tensor Jackson convolution is formed from the order-four Jackson kernel in \cref{obj:def:jackson-kernel}.
\item \textbf{(Continuity.)} The function \(f\) is continuous on the normalized cube \([-1,1]^D\).
\item \textbf{(Weighted increment modulus.)} For every shift \(\tau\in\mathbb R^D\),
\[
\left|f\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}-f(\cos\varphi)\right|
\le
L\sum_{i=1}^D\left(a_i|\tau_i|+b_i\tau_i^2\right),
\]
where cosine is applied coordinatewise.
\end{itemize}
Then
\[
\left|(\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\varphi)-f(\cos\varphi)\right|
\le
L\sum_{i=1}^D\left(\frac{32a_i}{K}+\frac{64b_i}{K^2}\right).
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:jackson-convolution-modulus}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item Let
\[
\mathfrak B_D=[-\pi,\pi]^D
\qquad\text{and}\qquad
\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)=\prod_{i=1}^D J_K(\tau_i),
\quad \tau\in\mathfrak B_D .
\]
For the positive integer \(K\) in the statement, \(J_K\) is the normalized order-four Jackson factor on \([-\pi,\pi]\), with removable values filled continuously. The raw fourth-power factor is nonnegative and has positive integral, so its normalized version satisfies \(J_K(\rho)\ge0\) for every \(\rho\in[-\pi,\pi]\). The tensor product kernel is normalized to have unit mass:
\[
\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\ge0,
\qquad
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
=
\prod_{i=1}^D\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}J_K(\rho)\,d\rho
=1.
\]
The convolution is written in the shifted-variable form
\[
(\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\varphi)
=
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
f\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau .
\]
When \(D=0\), the product is the empty product and the sums below are empty sums, so these identities keep their displayed values. Continuity of \(f\) on the normalized cube, coordinatewise continuity of cosine, and compactness of \(\mathfrak B_D\) give the integrability used below. % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.tensorJackson_integral_eq_one

\item The preceding unit-mass identity gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
(\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\varphi)-f(\cos\varphi)
&=
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
f\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
-
f(\cos\varphi)
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau  \\
&=
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
\bigl[
f\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}-f(\cos\varphi)
\bigr]\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau .
\end{aligned}
\]
The subtraction is justified by the compact-domain integrability noted above. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.tensorConvolution_approx_weighted

\item Taking absolute values, using nonnegativity of \(\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}\), and applying the assumed weighted increment modulus pointwise yields
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left|(\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\varphi)-f(\cos\varphi)\right|
&\le
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
\left|f\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}-f(\cos\varphi)\right|
\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau  \\
&\le
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
L\sum_{i=1}^D\left(a_i|\tau_i|+b_i\tau_i^2\right)
\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau .
\end{aligned}
\] % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.tensorConvolution_approx_weighted

\item Distributing the finite sum through the integral gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\int_{\mathfrak B_D}
L\sum_{i=1}^D\left(a_i|\tau_i|+b_i\tau_i^2\right)
\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau  \\
&\qquad =
L\sum_{i=1}^D
\left[
a_i\int_{\mathfrak B_D}|\tau_i|\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
+
b_i\int_{\mathfrak B_D}\tau_i^2\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
\right].
\end{aligned}
\]
For each selected coordinate \(i\), product integration and the one-dimensional unit mass give
\[
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}|\tau_i|\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
=
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|\rho|J_K(\rho)\,d\rho
\]
and
\[
\int_{\mathfrak B_D}\tau_i^2\mathfrak k_K^{(D)}(\tau)\,d\tau
=
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\rho^2J_K(\rho)\,d\rho .
\] % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.tensorJackson_first_moment_eq; Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.tensorJackson_second_moment_eq

\item Write the raw kernel and its mass as
\[
\widetilde J_K(\rho)=
\begin{cases}
K^4, & \sin(\rho/2)=0,\\[2mm]
\left\{\dfrac{\sin(K\rho/2)}{\sin(\rho/2)}\right\}^4,
& \sin(\rho/2)\ne0,
\end{cases}
\qquad
\mathfrak m_K=\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\widetilde J_K(\rho)\,d\rho,
\]
so that \(J_K(\rho)=\widetilde J_K(\rho)/\mathfrak m_K\). Let \(U_m\) denote the Chebyshev polynomial of the second kind. The removable-value convention and the identity
\[
U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}
=
\frac{\sin(K\rho/2)}{\sin(\rho/2)}
\]
away from the removable points give
\[
\widetilde J_K(\rho)
=
U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^4 .
\]
The square expansion
\[
U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^2
=
K+2\sum_{q\in\{0,\ldots,K-2\}}(K-1-q)\cos\{(q+1)\rho\}
\]
follows from the Chebyshev recurrence, with the displayed sum interpreted as an empty sum when \(K=1\). Using
\[
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\cos(r\rho)\,d\rho=0,
\qquad
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\cos(r\rho)\cos(s\rho)\,d\rho
=
\begin{cases}
\pi,& r=s,\\
0,& r\ne s,
\end{cases}
\]
for positive integers \(r,s\), one obtains
\[
\begin{aligned}
\mathfrak m_K
&=
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}
\left[
K+2\sum_{q\in\{0,\ldots,K-2\}}(K-1-q)\cos\{(q+1)\rho\}
\right]^2\,d\rho  \\
&=
2\pi K^2+4\pi\sum_{q\in\{0,\ldots,K-2\}}(K-1-q)^2  \\
&=
2\pi K^2+\frac{2\pi}{3}(K-1)K(2K-1)
=
\frac{2\pi}{3}K(2K^2+1).
\end{aligned}
\]
In particular,
\[
\mathfrak m_K\ge \frac{4\pi}{3}K^3>0 .
\] % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.jrawMass_eq

\item The same Chebyshev-square expansion and the vanishing of all positive-frequency cosine integrals give
\[
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}
U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^2\,d\rho
=
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}
\left[
K+2\sum_{q\in\{0,\ldots,K-2\}}(K-1-q)\cos\{(q+1)\rho\}
\right]d\rho
=
2\pi K .
\]
For \(\rho\in[-\pi,\pi]\),
\[
\rho^2\le \pi^2\sin^2(\rho/2).
\]
If \(\sin(\rho/2)=0\), then \(\rho=0\) on this interval and
\[
\rho^2\widetilde J_K(\rho)
\le
\pi^2U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^2 .
\]
If \(\sin(\rho/2)\ne0\), put
\[
U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}=\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)},
\qquad
s_K(\rho)=\sin(K\rho/2),
\qquad
d(\rho)=\sin(\rho/2).
\]
Since \(s_K(\rho)^2\le1\),
\[
\begin{aligned}
\rho^2\widetilde J_K(\rho)
&=
\rho^2\left\{\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)}\right\}^4  \\
&=
\rho^2\left\{\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)}\right\}^2
\left\{\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)}\right\}^2  \\
&\le
\pi^2s_K(\rho)^2
\left\{\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)}\right\}^2
\le
\pi^2
\left\{\frac{s_K(\rho)}{d(\rho)}\right\}^2
=
\pi^2U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^2 .
\end{aligned}
\]
After integration and normalization,
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\rho^2J_K(\rho)\,d\rho
&=
\frac{1}{\mathfrak m_K}
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\rho^2\widetilde J_K(\rho)\,d\rho  \\
&\le
\frac{\pi^2}{\mathfrak m_K}
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}U_{K-1}\{\cos(\rho/2)\}^2\,d\rho  \\
&\le
\frac{2\pi^3K}{(4\pi/3)K^3}
=
\frac{3\pi^2}{2K^2}
\le
\frac{24}{K^2}
\le
\frac{64}{K^2}.
\end{aligned}
\] % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.jackson_second_moment

\item The elementary inequality
\[
0\le (K|\rho|-2)^2
\]
is equivalent, since \(K>0\), to
\[
|\rho|\le \frac K4\rho^2+\frac1K .
\]
Multiplying by \(J_K(\rho)\ge0\), integrating, and using the preceding second-moment estimate together with \(\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}J_K(\rho)\,d\rho=1\), gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}|\rho|J_K(\rho)\,d\rho
&\le
\frac K4\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}\rho^2J_K(\rho)\,d\rho
+
\frac1K\int_{-\pi}^{\pi}J_K(\rho)\,d\rho  \\
&\le
\frac K4\frac{24}{K^2}+\frac1K
=
\frac7K
\le
\frac{32}{K}.
\end{aligned}
\] % lean: Causalean.Mathlib.Analysis.JacksonApproximation.jackson_first_moment

\item Combining the integral bound from the previous steps with the tensor moment reductions gives
\[
\begin{aligned}
\left|(\mathcal J_K f)(\cos\varphi)-f(\cos\varphi)\right|
&\le
L\sum_{i=1}^D
\left[
a_i\frac{32}{K}
+
b_i\frac{64}{K^2}
\right]  \\
&=
L\sum_{i=1}^D
\left(\frac{32a_i}{K}+\frac{64b_i}{K^2}\right).
\end{aligned}
\]
Here \(L\ge0\), \(a_i\ge0\), and \(b_i\ge0\) justify multiplying the one-dimensional moment inequalities and summing them coordinatewise. This is the asserted bound. % lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.tensorConvolution_approx_weighted
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

Combining the coefficient envelope with the angular modulus gives the single polynomial certificate used in the upper-bound proof. It applies uniformly to nonnegative rectangles with positive radii, including the pilot-local rectangles that may meet coordinate faces or the vertex of the overlap cone.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:simultaneous-jackson-certificate}[Simultaneous Jackson certificate]
There is a universal constant \(A>0\) such that, for every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\) with \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), there is a constant \(C_\epsilon>0\) with the following property. Let:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Degree.)} \(K\ge 2\).
\item \textbf{(Rectangle.)} \(Q_x=\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}[\ell_{\jmath,x},u_{\jmath,x}]\) have \(\ell_{\jmath,x}\le u_{\jmath,x}\), \(\ell_{\jmath,x}\ge 0\), and positive radii
\[
r_{\jmath,x}=\frac{u_{\jmath,x}-\ell_{\jmath,x}}{2}>0
\]
for every \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\).
\end{itemize}
Let \(P_{x,K}\) be the tensor Jackson polynomial associated with \(\epsilon\), \(K\), and \(Q_x\) in \cref{obj:def:jackson-kernel}. Then the same polynomial satisfies all of the following conclusions:
\[
\deg_{\jmath}(P_{x,K})\le 2(K-1)
\qquad\text{for every }\jmath\in\mathcal J .
\]
For every \(v\in Q_x\),
\[
\left|P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(v)\right|
\le
C_\epsilon
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left\{
\frac{\sqrt{(v_{\jmath}-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-v_{\jmath})}}{K}
+
\frac{r_{\jmath,x}}{K^2}
\right\}.
\]
There are a finite multi-index set \(\mathcal A_x\) and coefficients \(b_{x,\alpha}\) such that, for every \(v\),
\[
P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}
b_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left(\frac{v_{\jmath}-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right)^{\alpha_{\jmath}},
\]
with
\[
\alpha_{\jmath}\le 2(K-1)
\qquad\text{for every }\alpha\in\mathcal A_x\text{ and every }\jmath\in\mathcal J,
\]
and
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le
A^K(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J} r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:simultaneous-jackson-certificate}]
Take
\[
A=2^{60}.
\]
Fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\), and set
\[
C_\epsilon=32(1+\epsilon^{-1}).
\]
Let \(K\ge2\) and let \(Q_x\) satisfy the displayed hypotheses. Applying
\cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction} to this rectangle gives the tensor Jackson polynomial
\(P_{x,K}\) and a normalized centered polynomial \(\mathcal R_{x,K}\). The same result gives, first,
\[
\deg_{\jmath}(P_{x,K})\le 2(K-1)
\qquad(\jmath\in\mathcal J).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.jacksonTensorPolynomialData_exists

Fix \(v\in Q_x\). Choose a bijection \(\eta:\mathcal J\to\{1,2,3,4\}\), and write
\[
c_\omega=b_{\eta^{-1}(\omega),x},
\qquad
\rho_\omega=r_{\eta^{-1}(\omega),x},
\qquad
y_\omega=v_{\eta^{-1}(\omega)}
\qquad(\omega=1,\ldots,4).
\]
Define the normalized point and angle vector by
\[
z_\omega=\frac{y_\omega-c_\omega}{\rho_\omega},
\qquad
\varphi_\omega=\arccos z_\omega .
\]
Since \(v\in Q_x\) and every radius is positive, \(z\in[-1,1]^4\) and
\[
v_{\jmath}=b_{\jmath,x}+r_{\jmath,x}\cos\varphi_{\eta(\jmath)}
\qquad(\jmath\in\mathcal J).
\]
On \([-1,1]^4\), set
\[
F_x(w)
=
\bar f_\epsilon\!\left(
\bigl\{b_{\jmath,x}+r_{\jmath,x}w_{\eta(\jmath)}\bigr\}_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\right).
\]
The lower-endpoint hypothesis gives \(b_{\jmath,x}-r_{\jmath,x}=\ell_{\jmath,x}\ge0\), so the affine image of
\([-1,1]^4\) lies in \(\mathbb R_+^4\). The global Lipschitz property in
\cref{obj:prop:identification-and-extension} therefore implies that \(F_x\) is continuous on
\([-1,1]^4\) and that, for all \(w,w'\in[-1,1]^4\),
\[
|F_x(w)-F_x(w')|
\le
(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\omega=1}^4
\rho_\omega |w_\omega-w'_\omega|.
\]
For every shift \(\tau\in\mathbb R^4\), the elementary inequality
\[
|\cos(\varphi_\omega-\tau_\omega)-\cos\varphi_\omega|
\le
|\sin\varphi_\omega|\,|\tau_\omega|+\frac{\tau_\omega^2}{2}
\]
therefore gives
\[
\left|
F_x\{\cos(\varphi-\tau)\}-F_x(\cos\varphi)
\right|
\le
(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\omega=1}^4
\left(
\rho_\omega|\sin\varphi_\omega|\,|\tau_\omega|
+\frac{\rho_\omega}{2}\tau_\omega^2
\right).
\]
Applying \cref{obj:lem:jackson-convolution-modulus} with
\[
L=1+\epsilon^{-1},
\qquad
a_\omega=\rho_\omega|\sin\varphi_\omega|,
\qquad
b_\omega=\frac{\rho_\omega}{2}
\]
yields
\[
|P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(v)|
\le
(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\omega=1}^4
\left(
\frac{32\rho_\omega|\sin\varphi_\omega|}{K}
+
\frac{32\rho_\omega}{K^2}
\right),
\]
where the evaluation identity for \(P_{x,K}\) is the Jackson-convolution identity supplied by
\cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction}.
Finally, for each \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\),
\[
r_{\jmath,x}|\sin\varphi_{\eta(\jmath)}|
=
\sqrt{(v_{\jmath}-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-v_{\jmath})},
\]
because
\[
(v_{\jmath}-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-v_{\jmath})
=
r_{\jmath,x}^2
\left\{
1-\left(\frac{v_{\jmath}-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right)^2
\right\}.
\]
Reindexing the preceding sum over \(\mathcal J\) and using
\(C_\epsilon=32(1+\epsilon^{-1})\) gives the asserted pointwise bound.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.tensorConvolution_approx_weighted

It remains to record the centered expansion and its coefficient envelope. By
\cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction}, for every four-coordinate vector \(v\),
\[
P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\mathcal R_{x,K}\left(
\left\{\frac{v_{\jmath}-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right\}_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\right).
\]
Let \(\mathcal A_x\) be the image of \(\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal R_{x,K})\) under the coordinate reindexing
from \(\{1,2,3,4\}\) to \(\mathcal J\), and let \(b_{x,\alpha}\) be the corresponding reindexed coefficient.
Then the preceding display becomes
\[
P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}
b_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left(\frac{v_{\jmath}-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right)^{\alpha_{\jmath}}.
\]
The normalized coordinate-degree conclusion of \cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction} gives
\[
\alpha_{\jmath}\le 2(K-1)
\qquad
(\alpha\in\mathcal A_x,\ \jmath\in\mathcal J),
\]
and reindexing preserves the coefficient one-norm, so its coefficient envelope gives
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le
2^{40K+20}(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Since \(K\ge2\),
\[
40K+20\le60K,
\qquad
2^{40K+20}\le (2^{60})^K=A^K.
\]
Combining the last two displays proves the stated coefficient bound.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.simultaneous_jackson_certificate
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:simultaneous-jackson-certificate} is the analytic bridge between approximation and estimation. The pointwise bound controls the local bias of \(P_{x,K}\) on \(Q_x\), while the centered coefficient expansion controls the moments of the factorial estimator after replacing monomials by centered Poisson factorial polynomials.

We next record the pilot calculations that make the random rectangles usable. The good-pilot event is self-normalized: its radius is computed from the observed pilot count itself, so the event has the same scale in small and large cells.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:canonical-pilot-bad-moment}[Bad pilot moment bound]
Fix the four-coordinate set \(\mathcal J=\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}\). Let \(q=(q_\jmath)_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\) have nonnegative coordinates, let \(m>0\), let \(L\ge 1\), and let \(t\in\{1,2,4\}\). Suppose the pilot counts \((N'_{\jmath})_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\) are independent with
\[
N'_{\jmath}\sim \operatorname{Pois}(m q_\jmath),
\qquad \jmath\in\mathcal J .
\]
With \(H_0=1024\), define the normalized aggregate pilot score
\[
\Gamma(N')
=
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left[
\left|{N'_{\jmath}\over m}-q_\jmath\right|
+
H_0\left\{\sqrt{{N'_{\jmath}\over m}{L\over m}}+{L\over m}\right\}
\right],
\]
and define the self-normalized bad-pilot event
\[
\mathsf G^c
=
\left\{
\exists\,\jmath\in\mathcal J:
\left|{N'_{\jmath}\over m}-q_\jmath\right|
>
{H_0\over 4}
\left\{\sqrt{{N'_{\jmath}\over m}{L\over m}}+{L\over m}\right\}
\right\}.
\]
Then, with \(C_t=10^{80(t+1)}\),
\[
E\!\left[\Gamma(N')^t\mathbf 1_{\mathsf G^c}\right]
\le
C_t e^{-20L}
\left\{
\sqrt{{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)L\over m}}
+
{L\over m}
\right\}^{t}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:canonical-pilot-bad-moment}]
1.  For each \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\), define
\[
\eta_\jmath=mq_\jmath,\qquad
\delta_\jmath=\left|N'_\jmath-\eta_\jmath\right|,
\qquad
\rho_\jmath=\sqrt{N'_\jmath L}+L,
\qquad
\sigma_\jmath=\delta_\jmath+1024\rho_\jmath,
\]
and put
\[
\Sigma=\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\sigma_\jmath .
\]
Since \(m>0\),
\[
\left|{N'_\jmath\over m}-q_\jmath\right|
={\delta_\jmath\over m},
\qquad
\sqrt{{N'_\jmath\over m}{L\over m}}+{L\over m}
={\rho_\jmath\over m}.
\]
Thus
\[
\Gamma(N')={\Sigma\over m},
\qquad
\mathsf G^c
=
\bigcup_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\{\delta_\jmath>256\rho_\jmath\},
\]
and hence
\[
E\!\left[\Gamma(N')^t\mathbf 1_{\mathsf G^c}\right]
=
m^{-t}
E\!\left[
\Sigma^t
\mathbf 1_{\cup_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\{\delta_\jmath>256\rho_\jmath\}}
\right].
\]
% lean: Causalean.Stat.Concentration.PoissonSelfNormalized.independent_poisson_normalized_badAny_moment

2.  We first record the scalar estimate used in the product step.  Let
\(\mathsf W\sim\operatorname{Pois}(\Lambda)\), where \(\Lambda\ge0\), and define
\[
\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)=|\mathsf W-\Lambda|,
\qquad
\rho_L(\mathsf W)=\sqrt{\mathsf W L}+L,
\qquad
\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)
=
\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)+1024\rho_L(\mathsf W),
\]
\[
\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}=\sqrt{\Lambda L}+L,
\qquad
\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L}
=
\{\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)>256\rho_L(\mathsf W)\}.
\]
For every real \(\xi\),
\[
E\exp\{\xi(\mathsf W-\Lambda)\}
=
\exp\{\Lambda(e^\xi-1-\xi)\}.
\]
The Chernoff--Bennett bounds obtained from this identity give, for every
\(\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}\ge0\),
\[
\Pr\{\mathsf W-\Lambda>\sqrt{2\Lambda\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}}
       +2\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}\}\le e^{-\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}},
\qquad
\Pr\{\Lambda-\mathsf W>\sqrt{2\Lambda\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}}\}
\le e^{-\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}}.
\]
With \(\zeta_{\mathrm{tail}}=80L\),
\[
\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L}
\subseteq
\{\mathsf W-\Lambda>\sqrt{160\Lambda L}+160L\}
\cup
\{\Lambda-\mathsf W>\sqrt{160\Lambda L}\}.
\]
Indeed, on \(\{\Lambda\le\mathsf W\}\),
\[
\sqrt{160\Lambda L}\le16\sqrt{\mathsf W L},
\]
so the bad-event inequality implies the displayed upper-tail event.  On
\(\{\mathsf W<\Lambda\}\), the inequality
\[
\Lambda-\mathsf W>256(\sqrt{\mathsf W L}+L)
\]
implies
\[
(\Lambda-\mathsf W)^2-160L(\Lambda-\mathsf W)-160\mathsf W L>0,
\]
and therefore \((\Lambda-\mathsf W)^2>160\Lambda L\), which is the displayed
lower-tail event.  Consequently
\[
\Pr(\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L})\le 2e^{-80L}.
\]

Next,
\[
\sqrt{\mathsf W L}
\le
\sqrt{\Lambda L}+\sqrt{\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)L}
\le
\sqrt{\Lambda L}+{\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)+L\over2},
\]
and hence
\[
\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)
\le
1536\{\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}+\delta_{\Lambda}(\mathsf W)\}.
\]
Since \(L\ge1\), \(\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}\ge1\).  Set
\[
\xi_{\Lambda,L}={1\over 2\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}}.
\]
Then \(|\xi_{\Lambda,L}|\le1\) and
\(\Lambda\xi_{\Lambda,L}^2\le1/4\).  The preceding deterministic bound gives
\[
\exp\!\left\{
{\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)\over4096\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}}
\right\}
\le
e^{1/2}
\left[
e^{\xi_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W-\Lambda)}
+
e^{-\xi_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W-\Lambda)}
\right].
\]
Using the moment-generating identity and
\(|e^r-1-r|\le r^2\) for \(|r|\le1\),
\[
E\exp\!\left\{
{\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)\over4096\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}}
\right\}
\le
e^{1/2}(e^{1/4}+e^{1/4})
\le 8.
\]
The elementary exponential domination of powers then yields, for every integer
\(s\) with \(0\le s\le8\),
\[
E\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)^s
\le
10^{4s+4}\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}^{\,s}.
\]
For \(t\in\{1,2,4\}\), define
\[
\zeta_{\Lambda,L,t}
=
e^{-40L}\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}^{-t}.
\]
With
\[
X_{\Lambda,L,t}(\mathsf W)=\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)^t,
\]
the pointwise Young inequality gives
\[
\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L}}X_{\Lambda,L,t}
\le
{ \zeta_{\Lambda,L,t}X_{\Lambda,L,t}^2
+\zeta_{\Lambda,L,t}^{-1}\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L}}
\over2}.
\]
Combining the moment bound with \(s=2t\) and the bad-event probability bound,
\[
E\!\left[
\sigma_{\Lambda,L}(\mathsf W)^t\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_{\Lambda,L}}
\right]
\le
{10^{8t+4}+2\over2}\,
e^{-40L}\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}^{\,t}
\le
10^{8t+8}e^{-40L}\upsilon_{\Lambda,L}^{\,t}.
\]
% lean: Causalean.Stat.Concentration.PoissonSelfNormalized.poisson_weighted_bad_moment

3.  Now let \(\mathcal I\) be a finite coordinate set with
\(|\mathcal I|\le4\), and let
\((\mathsf W_\iota)_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\) be independent with
\(\mathsf W_\iota\sim\operatorname{Pois}(\Lambda_\iota)\).  Define
\[
\delta_\iota=|\mathsf W_\iota-\Lambda_\iota|,
\qquad
\rho_\iota=\sqrt{\mathsf W_\iota L}+L,
\qquad
\sigma_\iota=\delta_\iota+1024\rho_\iota,
\]
\[
\mathcal B_\iota=\{\delta_\iota>256\rho_\iota\},
\qquad
\Sigma_{\mathcal I}=\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\sigma_\iota,
\qquad
\Xi_{\mathcal I,L}
=
\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\Lambda_\iota\right)L}+L .
\]
If \(\mathcal I=\varnothing\), the corresponding integral is zero.  Otherwise,
since each \(\sigma_\iota\ge0\),
\[
\Sigma_{\mathcal I}^t
\le
|\mathcal I|^{t-1}
\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\sigma_\iota^t ,
\]
for \(t\in\{1,2,4\}\), and therefore
\[
E\!\left[
\Sigma_{\mathcal I}^t
\mathbf 1_{\cup_{\iota'\in\mathcal I}\mathcal B_{\iota'}}
\right]
\le
|\mathcal I|^{t-1}
\sum_{\iota',\iota\in\mathcal I}
E\!\left[
\sigma_\iota^t\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_{\iota'}}
\right].
\]
When \(\iota=\iota'\), the scalar bound from step 2 gives
\[
E\!\left[\sigma_\iota^t\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_\iota}\right]
\le
10^{8t+8}e^{-40L}
\left(\sqrt{\Lambda_\iota L}+L\right)^t.
\]
When \(\iota\ne\iota'\), independence factors the expectation and the scalar
moment and probability bounds give
\[
E\!\left[
\sigma_\iota^t\mathbf 1_{\mathcal B_{\iota'}}
\right]
=
E\sigma_\iota^t\,\Pr(\mathcal B_{\iota'})
\le
2\cdot10^{4t+4}e^{-40L}
\left(\sqrt{\Lambda_\iota L}+L\right)^t.
\]
Thus, with
\[
\mathfrak c_t=10^{8t+8}+2\cdot10^{4t+4},
\]
\[
E\!\left[
\Sigma_{\mathcal I}^t
\mathbf 1_{\cup_{\iota'\in\mathcal I}\mathcal B_{\iota'}}
\right]
\le
|\mathcal I|^t\mathfrak c_t e^{-40L}
\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}
\left(\sqrt{\Lambda_\iota L}+L\right)^t .
\]
Cauchy--Schwarz gives
\[
\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\sqrt{\Lambda_\iota L}
\le
|\mathcal I|
\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\Lambda_\iota\right)L},
\]
and hence
\[
\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}
\left(\sqrt{\Lambda_\iota L}+L\right)^t
\le
|\mathcal I|^{t+1}\Xi_{\mathcal I,L}^{\,t}.
\]
Since \(|\mathcal I|\le4\),
\[
|\mathcal I|^{2t+1}\mathfrak c_t
\le
10^{16(4+1)(t+1)}
=
10^{80(t+1)}.
\]
Also \(e^{-40L}\le e^{-20L}\).  Therefore
\[
E\!\left[
\Sigma_{\mathcal I}^t
\mathbf 1_{\cup_{\iota'\in\mathcal I}\mathcal B_{\iota'}}
\right]
\le
10^{80(t+1)}e^{-20L}
\left\{
\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\iota\in\mathcal I}\Lambda_\iota\right)L}+L
\right\}^{t}.
\]
% lean: Causalean.Stat.Concentration.PoissonSelfNormalized.independent_poisson_badAny_moment

4.  Apply the preceding product bound with
\[
\mathcal I=\mathcal J,
\qquad
\Lambda_\jmath=mq_\jmath .
\]
The product-Poisson pilot law supplies the displayed coordinate laws and
independence, and \(|\mathcal J|=4\).  Hence
\[
E\!\left[
\Sigma^t
\mathbf 1_{\cup_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\{\delta_\jmath>256\rho_\jmath\}}
\right]
\le
10^{80(t+1)}e^{-20L}
\left\{
\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}mq_\jmath\right)L}+L
\right\}^{t}.
\]
Combining this estimate with step 1 and using \(m>0\),
\[
m^{-1}
\left\{
\sqrt{m\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)L}+L
\right\}
=
\sqrt{{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)L\over m}}
+
{L\over m}.
\]
Therefore
\[
E\!\left[\Gamma(N')^t\mathbf 1_{\mathsf G^c}\right]
\le
10^{80(t+1)}e^{-20L}
\left\{
\sqrt{{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)L\over m}}
+
{L\over m}
\right\}^{t}.
\]
Since \(C_t=10^{80(t+1)}\), this is the asserted bound.
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.canonicalPilotBadMoment
\end{proof}

The exponential factor in \cref{obj:lem:canonical-pilot-bad-moment} is paired with the logarithmic choice \(L_d=\log(ed)\) from \cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator}. This pairing makes the contribution from bad pilot rectangles summable across cells at the rate used in \cref{obj:thm:jackson-factorial-upper}.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_12}[Good-pilot event]
Fix a cell \(x\), four-cell mass vector \(q_x=(q_{\jmath,x})_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\), Poisson scale \(m>0\), alphabet logarithm \(L_d=\log(ed)\), pilot-radius constant \(H_0\), and pilot counts \((N'_{\jmath,x})_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\). Put \(c_{\jmath,x}=N'_{\jmath,x}/m\) and \(h_{\jmath,x}=H_0\{\sqrt{c_{\jmath,x}L_d/m}+L_d/m\}\). The coordinatewise good-pilot event used for the pilot rectangle is \[\mathsf G_x:=\bigcap_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\left\{\left|\frac{N'_{\jmath,x}}{m}-q_{\jmath,x}\right|\le \frac{h_{\jmath,x}}{4}\right\}.\]
\end{definitionv}

On \(\mathsf G_x\), the pilot rectangle radius is comparable to the local Poisson scale. The next lemma expresses that comparison in the aggregate form needed by the coefficient envelope.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:good-pilot-radius-sum}[Good pilot radius bound]
Fix \(m>0\), an integer \(d\ge 1\), pilot counts \(N'_{\jmath,x}\) for \(\jmath\) in the four-coordinate set \(\mathcal J\), and coordinate intensities \(q_\jmath\ge 0\).  Let \(L_d=\log(ed)\), set \(p_x=\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\), and let \(Q_x=\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}[\ell_{\jmath,x},u_{\jmath,x}]\) be the canonical pilot rectangle of \cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator}, with radii
\[
r_{\jmath,x}=\frac{u_{\jmath,x}-\ell_{\jmath,x}}{2}.
\]
Assume that the good-pilot event
\[
\mathsf G_x=\bigcap_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left\{\left|c_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath\right|\le \frac{h_{\jmath,x}}{4}\right\},
\qquad c_{\jmath,x}=\frac{N'_{\jmath,x}}{m},
\]
holds, where \(h_{\jmath,x}\) is the canonical pilot half-width in \cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator}.  Then
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J} r_{\jmath,x}
\le
2\cdot 10^6
\left\{
\sqrt{\frac{p_x L_d}{m}}+\frac{L_d}{m}
\right\}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:good-pilot-radius-sum}]
Fix \(x\) and set
\[
\zeta=\frac{L_d}{m}.
\]
Since \(d\ge 1\), \(ed\ge e>1\), so \(L_d=\log(ed)>0\). Hence \(\zeta>0\). For each \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\), write
\[
\sigma_{\jmath,x}=\sqrt{c_{\jmath,x}\zeta}.
\]
For the canonical tuning used in this lemma, the pilot half-width is calibrated with \(H_0=1024\). Hence
\[
h_{\jmath,x}=1024(\sigma_{\jmath,x}+\zeta).
\]
Also \(\sigma_{\jmath,x}\ge0\), and
\[
\sigma_{\jmath,x}\le \frac{c_{\jmath,x}}{512}+128\zeta
\]
because both sides are nonnegative and
\[
\left(\frac{c_{\jmath,x}}{512}+128\zeta\right)^2-c_{\jmath,x}\zeta
=
\left(\frac{c_{\jmath,x}}{512}-128\zeta\right)^2\ge0 .
\]
The good-pilot event gives
\[
c_{\jmath,x}\le q_\jmath+\frac{h_{\jmath,x}}4 .
\]
Substituting the displayed formula for \(h_{\jmath,x}\) and then using the preceding bound on
\(\sigma_{\jmath,x}\),
\[
c_{\jmath,x}
\le q_\jmath+256\sigma_{\jmath,x}+256\zeta
\le q_\jmath+\frac{c_{\jmath,x}}2+33024\zeta .
\]
Thus
\begin{equation}\label{eq:good-pilot-radius-center-bound}
c_{\jmath,x}\le 2q_\jmath+66048\zeta .
\end{equation}
% lean: canonicalPilotGoodRadiusSum_le

Define
\[
\rho_{\jmath,x}=\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}.
\]
Since \(q_\jmath\ge0\) and \(\zeta>0\), we have \(\rho_{\jmath,x}^2=q_\jmath\zeta\). From \cref{eq:good-pilot-radius-center-bound},
\[
\sigma_{\jmath,x}^2
=
c_{\jmath,x}\zeta
\le
(2q_\jmath+66048\zeta)\zeta .
\]
Moreover,
\[
(2\rho_{\jmath,x}+300\zeta)^2-(2q_\jmath+66048\zeta)\zeta
=
2q_\jmath\zeta+1200\rho_{\jmath,x}\zeta+23952\zeta^2
\ge0 .
\]
Taking square roots gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:good-pilot-radius-sigma-bound}
\sigma_{\jmath,x}\le 2\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}+300\zeta .
\end{equation}
% lean: canonicalPilotGoodRadiusSum_le

For the zero-truncated pilot interval,
\[
r_{\jmath,x}
=
\frac{c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x}-\max\{0,c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}\}}2 .
\]
If \(c_{\jmath,x}\le h_{\jmath,x}\), then \(r_{\jmath,x}=(c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x})/2\le h_{\jmath,x}\). If
\(c_{\jmath,x}>h_{\jmath,x}\), then \(r_{\jmath,x}=h_{\jmath,x}\). Therefore
\[
r_{\jmath,x}\le h_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Combining this with the formula for \(h_{\jmath,x}\) and \cref{eq:good-pilot-radius-sigma-bound},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:good-pilot-radius-coordinate-bound}
r_{\jmath,x}
\le
1024(\sigma_{\jmath,x}+\zeta)
\le
2048\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}+308224\zeta .
\end{equation}
% lean: pilotRectangle_radius_bounds

Summing \cref{eq:good-pilot-radius-coordinate-bound} over the four coordinates,
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}
\le
2048\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}
+
4\cdot 308224\,\zeta .
\]
By Cauchy's inequality on the four-point set \(\mathcal J\),
\[
\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}\right)^2
\le
4\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\bigl(\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}\bigr)^2
=
4\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)\zeta .
\]
All terms are nonnegative, so
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\sqrt{q_\jmath\zeta}
\le
2\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)\zeta}.
\]
Consequently,
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}
\le
4096\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)\zeta}
+
1232896\,\zeta
\le
2\cdot 10^6
\left\{
\sqrt{\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\right)\zeta}
+\zeta
\right\}.
\]
Since \(p_x=\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath\) and \(\zeta=L_d/m\), the desired bound follows:
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J} r_{\jmath,x}
\le
2\cdot 10^6
\left\{
\sqrt{\frac{p_xL_d}{m}}+\frac{L_d}{m}
\right\}.
\]
% lean: canonicalPilotGoodRadiusSum_le
\end{proof}

The radius bound turns the abstract coefficient control into a stochastic bound indexed by \(p_x\), the cell mass. The following normalized envelope is the version applied directly to the centered polynomial expansion inside the estimator.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope}[Normalized coefficient envelope]
Let \(\epsilon>0\) be the overlap parameter from \cref{obj:ass:fixed-overlap}, let \(K\ge2\) be an integer, and let \(Q_x=\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}[\ell_{\jmath,x},u_{\jmath,x}]\) be a rectangle over the four outcome-arm coordinate set \(\mathcal J\). Suppose that:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Ordered endpoints.)} \(\ell_{\jmath,x}\le u_{\jmath,x}\) for every \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\).
\item \textbf{(Nonnegative lower endpoints.)} \(\ell_{\jmath,x}\ge0\) for every \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\).
\item \textbf{(Positive radii.)} With
\[
b_{\jmath,x}=\frac{\ell_{\jmath,x}+u_{\jmath,x}}{2},
\qquad
r_{\jmath,x}=\frac{u_{\jmath,x}-\ell_{\jmath,x}}{2},
\]
one has \(r_{\jmath,x}>0\) for every \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\).
\end{itemize}
Let \(b_x=(b_{\jmath,x})_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\), let \(r_x=(r_{\jmath,x})_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\), and let \(P_{x,K}\) be the Jackson tensor polynomial attached to \(Q_x\) by \cref{obj:def:jackson-kernel}. Define
\[
\mathcal R_{x,K}(z)
=
P_{x,K}\bigl((b_{\jmath,x}+r_{\jmath,x}z_\jmath)_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\bigr)
-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x} b_{x,\alpha}\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J} z_\jmath^{\alpha_\jmath},
\]
where \(\bar f_\epsilon\) is the global cell extension from \cref{obj:def:global-extension} and \(\mathcal A_x=\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal R_{x,K})\). Then
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le
(2^{60})^{K}(1+\epsilon^{-1})\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Moreover, every \(\alpha\in\mathcal A_x\) satisfies
\[
\alpha_\jmath\le 2(K-1)
\]
for every \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\).
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item The ordered endpoints make \(Q_x\) a valid rectangle, and the remaining hypotheses give the lower-endpoint, positive-overlap, degree, and positive-radius conditions needed for \cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction}. Apply that result to \(Q_x\). Under the same positive-overlap and positive-radius conditions, the construction in \cref{obj:def:jackson-kernel} identifies the extracted physical polynomial with \(P_{x,K}\).

Fix a bijection
\[
\iota_{\mathcal J}:\mathcal J\longrightarrow \{1,2,3,4\}.
\]
Write the normalized polynomial supplied by \cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction} as
\[
\mathcal Q_{x,K}(\zeta)
=
\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal B_x}
\eta_\beta
\prod_{\varpi=1}^4 \zeta_\varpi^{\beta_\varpi},
\qquad
\mathcal B_x=\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal Q_{x,K})
\subset \mathbb N^{\{1,2,3,4\}},
\qquad
\eta_\beta=(\mathcal Q_{x,K})_\beta
\quad(\beta\in\mathcal B_x).
\]
Define the coordinate transport
\[
\Phi:\mathbb N^{\{1,2,3,4\}}\longrightarrow \mathbb N^{\mathcal J},
\qquad
(\Phi\beta)_\jmath=\beta_{\iota_{\mathcal J}(\jmath)},
\]
and set
\[
\mathcal S_x=\Phi(\mathcal B_x),
\qquad
\xi_{x,\alpha}=\eta_{\Phi^{-1}(\alpha)}
\quad\text{for }\alpha\in\mathcal S_x.
\]
Then, for every \(v\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal J}\),
\[
P_{x,K}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal S_x}
\xi_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left(\frac{v_\jmath-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right)^{\alpha_\jmath}.
\]
The coefficient sum is unchanged by this relabeling:
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal S_x}|\xi_{x,\alpha}|
=
\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal B_x}|\eta_\beta|.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.jacksonTensorPolynomialData_exists

\item\label{proof:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope:normalized-expansion} Substitute \(v_\jmath=b_{\jmath,x}+r_{\jmath,x}z_\jmath\) in the preceding display. Since every \(r_{\jmath,x}>0\),
\[
\frac{b_{\jmath,x}+r_{\jmath,x}z_\jmath-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}
=
z_\jmath .
\]
Thus, as a polynomial in \(z\in\mathbb R^{\mathcal J}\),
\[
\mathcal R_{x,K}(z)
=
P_{x,K}(b_x+r_x\odot z)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal S_x}
\xi_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}z_\jmath^{\alpha_\jmath}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.centeredNormalizedPolynomial_eq_of_expansion

\item The last identity implies that every coefficient of \(\mathcal R_{x,K}\) outside \(\mathcal S_x\) is zero, and that for \(\alpha\in\mathcal A_x=\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal R_{x,K})\) the coefficient \(b_{x,\alpha}\) equals \(\xi_{x,\alpha}\). Hence
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal S_x}|\xi_{x,\alpha}|.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.centeredNormalizedPolynomial_coeffL1_le_of_expansion

\item By the coefficient-envelope part of \cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction},
\[
\sum_{\beta\in\mathcal B_x}|\eta_\beta|
\le
2^{40K+20}(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Since \(K\ge2\),
\[
40K+20\le 60K,
\qquad
2^{40K+20}\le 2^{60K}=(2^{60})^K.
\]
Also \((1+\epsilon^{-1})\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}\ge0\), because \(\epsilon>0\) and every radius is positive. Combining these inequalities with the relabeling identity gives
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le
(2^{60})^{K}(1+\epsilon^{-1})
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.jacksonCenteredNormalizedPolynomial_coeffL1_le

\item It remains to record the coordinate-degree bound. Let \(\alpha\in\mathcal A_x\). If \(\alpha\notin\mathcal S_x\), the polynomial identity in \cref{proof:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope:normalized-expansion} gives \(b_{x,\alpha}=0\), contradicting \(\alpha\in\operatorname{supp}(\mathcal R_{x,K})\). Hence \(\alpha=\Phi\beta\) for some \(\beta\in\mathcal B_x\). The normalized-degree part of \cref{obj:lem:jackson-tensor-extraction} gives
\[
\beta_\varpi\le 2(K-1)
\qquad\text{for every }\varpi\in\{1,2,3,4\}.
\]
Therefore, for each \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\),
\[
\alpha_\jmath
=
(\Phi\beta)_\jmath
=
\beta_{\iota_{\mathcal J}(\jmath)}
\le
2(K-1).
\]
% lean: CausalSmith.Stat.DiscreteOptimalValueMinimaxMatched.jacksonCenteredNormalizedPolynomial_coeffL1_le
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

The centered form in \cref{obj:lem:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope} aligns with the polynomial \(U_h(N;z)\) from \cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator}. This is the same unbiased polynomial-lifting principle used for nonsmooth functional estimation under Poisson sampling \citep{JiaoVenkatHanWeissman2015Functionals}.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_5}[Pilot-failure contributions]
Fix a cell \(x\), a four-cell mass vector \(q_x\), Poisson scale \(m>0\), and Jackson tuning. Let \(\mathbb P_{m,q_x}^{\mathrm{pil,eval}}\) denote the product-Poisson law of the pilot and evaluation count vectors, and let \(\mathsf G_x\) be the coordinatewise good-pilot event used for the pilot rectangle in the Jackson--factorial construction. With \(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}\) denoting the clipped cell statistic built from the pilot and evaluation counts, define \[ \mathrm{PF}_{1,x}:=\int_{\mathsf G_x^c}\left|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\right|\,d\mathbb P_{m,q_x}^{\mathrm{pil,eval}}, \qquad \mathrm{PF}_{2,x}:=\int_{\mathsf G_x^c}\left(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\right)^2\,d\mathbb P_{m,q_x}^{\mathrm{pil,eval}}. \]
\end{definitionv}

The quantities in \cref{obj:synth_5} separate the contribution of pilot failures from the conditional bias and variance calculations on \(\mathsf G_x\). The final lemma assembles the distributional identities, the conditional factorial moments, and the resulting cellwise controls.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:centered-factorial-pilot-control}[Centered factorial pilot control]
Assume the product experiment satisfies the i.i.d. sampling law in \cref{obj:ass:iid-sampling}.  There is a universal Jackson tuning with self-normalized Poisson pilot-radius constant, polynomial-degree constant \(\kappa=1/100000\), and cutoff \(D_0=3\).  For every overlap parameter \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\), there is a constant \(C_\epsilon>0\) such that the following assertions hold for every \(n,d\), every observed law \(\mathbb P\) on \([d]\times\{0,1\}^2\), and every positive sample size \(n\), whenever \(\mathbb P\in\mathcal D_{d,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) as in \cref{obj:def:observed-model-class}.  Set \(m=n/8\), let \(q_x=(q_{00,x},q_{01,x},q_{10,x},q_{11,x})\) be the four-coordinate cell vector, and let \(p_x\) be the marginal mass of cell \(x\).

In the uncapped marked experiment generated by
\[
M\sim \operatorname{Pois}(n/4)
\]
together with i.i.d. observations from \(\mathbb P\) and independent fair pilot/evaluation marks, the count \(M\) has law \(\operatorname{Pois}(n/4)\).  The induced pilot/evaluation table has law equal to the product, over cells \(x\in[d]\) and coordinates \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\), of two independent Poisson count arrays with coordinate means \(m q_{\jmath,x}\).  For each fixed cell \(x\), the marginal law of the pilot and evaluation counts in that cell is therefore
\[
\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath,x})
\otimes
\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath,x}).
\]

For every cell \(x\), every fixed pilot table, every coordinate \(\jmath\in\mathcal J\), every \(z\in\mathbb R\), and every \(h,t\in\mathbb N\), conditional expectation over the evaluation counts satisfies
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[U_h(N_{\jmath,x};z)\mid N'\right]
=
(q_{\jmath,x}-z)^h
\]
and
\[
\mathbb E\!\left[U_h(N_{\jmath,x};z)U_t(N_{\jmath,x};z)\mid N'\right]
=
\sum_{\ell=0}^{\min\{h,t\}}
\binom{h}{\ell}\binom{t}{\ell}\ell!
\left(\frac{q_{\jmath,x}}{m}\right)^\ell
(q_{\jmath,x}-z)^{h+t-2\ell}.
\]

With the same tuning, the clipped Jackson-factorial cell statistic \(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}\) obeys, uniformly over all cells \(x\in[d]\),
\[
\left|
\mathbb E T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)
\right|
\le
C_\epsilon
\left\{
\sqrt{\frac{p_x}{mL_d}}+\frac{1}{mL_d}
\right\},
\]
\[
\operatorname{Var}(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}})
\le
C_\epsilon d^{1/16}
\left\{
\frac{p_x L_d}{m}+\frac{L_d^2}{m^2}
\right\},
\]
and the pilot-failure contributions satisfy
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}
\le
C_\epsilon
\left\{
\sqrt{\frac{p_x}{mL_d}}+\frac{1}{mL_d}
\right\},
\qquad
\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}
\le
C_\epsilon d^{1/16}
\left\{
\frac{p_x L_d}{m}+\frac{L_d^2}{m^2}
\right\}.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:centered-factorial-pilot-control}]
\begin{enumerate}
\item \emph{Tuning and constants.}
Use the canonical Jackson tuning specified in the statement, whose numerical components are
\[
H_0=1024,\qquad \kappa=\frac1{100000},\qquad D_0=3 .
\]
Fix \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\) and set
\[
F=1+\epsilon^{-1}.
\]
Let \(C_J>0\) be the constant supplied by \cref{obj:lem:simultaneous-jackson-certificate}, and let
\(C_1,C_2>0\) be the \(t=1\) and \(t=2\) constants in
\cref{obj:lem:canonical-pilot-bad-moment}.  Define
\[
\begin{aligned}
C_\epsilon
={}&1+\{5\cdot10^{16}C_J+450000000\,e^{1000}F\}+450FC_1\\
&\quad+8e^{1000}F^2(2\cdot10^6)^2+8F^2C_2 .
\end{aligned}
\]
All summands after the leading \(1\) are nonnegative, so \(C_\epsilon>0\).
% lean: centered_factorial_pilot_control

\item \emph{Cell notation.}
Fix \(n,d,\mathbb P\), assume \(n>0\) and
\(\mathbb P\in\mathcal D_{d,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) as in
\cref{obj:def:observed-model-class}, and put
\[
m=\frac n8,\qquad L=L_d=\log(ed),\qquad
\mathcal J=\{0,1\}\times\{0,1\}.
\]
Then \(m>0\).  If \([d]\) is empty, the cellwise assertions are vacuous.  Otherwise fix
\(x\in[d]\).  Write
\[
q_\jmath=q_{\jmath,x},\qquad
q_x=(q_\jmath)_{\jmath\in\mathcal J},\qquad
p_x=\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}q_\jmath .
\]
The coordinates satisfy \(q_\jmath\ge0\).  Since \(x\in[d]\), \(d\ge1\), and hence
\[
L=\log(ed)=1+\log d\ge1,\qquad L>0 .
\]
% lean: centered_factorial_pilot_control

\item \emph{Poisson splitting.}
The uncapped experiment draws \(M\sim\operatorname{Pois}(n/4)\), giving the asserted marginal law of \(M\).  Conditional on inclusion, an observation in atom \((x',\jmath)\in[d]\times\mathcal J\) is independently marked pilot or evaluation with probability \(1/2\).  Since \(n/4=2m\), Poisson thinning gives pilot and evaluation intensities
\[
2m\,q_{\jmath,x'}\cdot\frac12=mq_{\jmath,x'}.
\]
Thus
\[
\operatorname{Law}\{(N'_{\jmath,x'},N_{\jmath,x'})_{x',\jmath}\}
=
\bigotimes_{x'\in[d]}\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath,x'})
\otimes
\bigotimes_{x'\in[d]}\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath,x'}) .
\]
Projecting this product law to the fixed cell \(x\) gives
\[
\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath})
\otimes
\bigotimes_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_{\jmath}) .
\]
% lean: uncappedMarkedCountLaw_count
% lean: uncappedMarkedCountLaw_table
% lean: pilotEvaluationTableLaw_cell

\item \label{item:pf-centered-factorial-moments}\emph{Centered factorial moments.}
By \cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator},
\[
U_h(N;z)=\sum_{a=0}^{h}\binom ha(-z)^{h-a}\frac{(N)_a}{m^a}.
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\sum_{h=0}^{\infty}U_h(N;z)\frac{w^h}{h!}
=
e^{-zw}\left(1+\frac wm\right)^N .
\]
For \(N\sim\operatorname{Pois}(mq)\),
\[
E\left[e^{-zw}\left(1+\frac wm\right)^N\right]
=
e^{(q-z)w},
\]
so coefficient comparison gives \(E\,U_h(N;z)=(q-z)^h\).  With two formal variables,
\[
E\left[
e^{-z(w_1+w_2)}
\left(1+\frac {w_1}m\right)^N
\left(1+\frac {w_2}m\right)^N
\right]
=
\exp\left\{(q-z)(w_1+w_2)+\frac qm w_1w_2\right\}.
\]
The coefficient of \(w_1^h w_2^t/(h!t!)\) is therefore
\[
\sum_{\ell=0}^{\min\{h,t\}}
\binom h\ell\binom t\ell\ell!
\left(\frac qm\right)^\ell
(q-z)^{h+t-2\ell}.
\]
The evaluation counts are independent of the pilot counts, so with
\(q=q_{\jmath,x}\) both identities hold conditionally on every fixed pilot table.
% lean: conditionalEvaluationExpectation_centeredFactorial
% lean: conditionalEvaluationExpectation_centeredFactorial_mul

\item \label{item:pf-pilot-geometry-failure}\emph{Pilot geometry and failure terms.}
For a fixed pilot table, define
\[
c_{\jmath,x}=\frac{N'_{\jmath,x}}m,\qquad
h_{\jmath,x}=1024\left\{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\jmath,x}L}{m}}+\frac Lm\right\},
\]
\[
\ell_{\jmath,x}=\max\{0,c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}\},\qquad
u_{\jmath,x}=c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x},
\]
and
\[
b_{\jmath,x}=\frac{\ell_{\jmath,x}+u_{\jmath,x}}2,\qquad
r_{\jmath,x}=\frac{u_{\jmath,x}-\ell_{\jmath,x}}2 .
\]
Let
\[
Q_x=\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}[\ell_{\jmath,x},u_{\jmath,x}],
\qquad
S_x=F\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x},
\qquad
\varrho_x=d^{1/4}S_x,
\]
and define the good-pilot event
\[
\mathsf G_x
=
\bigcap_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left\{
|c_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath|\le \frac14h_{\jmath,x}
\right\}.
\]
On \(\mathsf G_x\), \(q_x\in Q_x\): if \(c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}\le0\), then
\(\ell_{\jmath,x}=0\le q_\jmath\) and
\(q_\jmath\le c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x}/4\le u_{\jmath,x}\); if
\(c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}>0\), then
\(c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}\le q_\jmath\le c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x}\).
Also \(r_{\jmath,x}>0\) and
\[
\frac12h_{\jmath,x}\le r_{\jmath,x}\le h_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Define
\[
\Gamma_x(N')
=
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left[
|c_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath|
+
1024\left\{\sqrt{\frac{c_{\jmath,x}L}{m}}+\frac Lm\right\}
\right].
\]
The interval definitions give
\[
\|b_x-q_x\|_1+\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}
\le 2\Gamma_x(N').
\]
Using the notation of \cref{obj:def:overlap-cone,obj:def:global-extension}, for any two nonnegative four-vectors \(u,v\), the global cell extension satisfies the four-coordinate Lipschitz bound
\[
|\bar f_\epsilon(u)-\bar f_\epsilon(v)|
\le
F\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}|u_\jmath-v_\jmath|.
\]
For a fixed arm \(a\), write
\[
\rho_a(w)=\max\{s_a(w),\epsilon s(w)\},
\]
so \(g_{a,\epsilon}(w)=0\) when \(s(w)=0\), and
\(g_{a,\epsilon}(w)=s(w)w_{a1}/\rho_a(w)\) otherwise.  If one of
\(s(u),s(v)\) is zero, all coordinates of that vector vanish and
\(0\le g_{a,\epsilon}(w)\le s(w)\) gives the bound.  On the positive-mass part, split according to whether
\(s_a(w)\le\epsilon s(w)\) or \(s_a(w)\ge\epsilon s(w)\) for \(w=u,v\).  In the threshold-threshold case the arm value is
\(w_{a1}/\epsilon\).  In the arm-active case use
\[
\left|\frac{u_{a1}}{s_a(u)}-\frac{v_{a1}}{s_a(v)}\right|
\le
\frac{|u_{a1}-v_{a1}|+|u_{a0}-v_{a0}|}{s_a(u)}
\]
and \(s(u)/s_a(u)\le\epsilon^{-1}\).  In the two mixed cases, the nonnegative gap between \(s_a(w)\) and \(\epsilon s(w)\) is bounded by the corresponding changes in \(s_a\) and \(s\), hence by
\(\sum_{\jmath}|u_\jmath-v_\jmath|\); the preceding estimates give the same \(F\)-Lipschitz bound for each \(g_{a,\epsilon}\).  Taking the maximum over the two arms preserves this bound.  The clipping interval in
\cref{obj:def:jackson-factorial-estimator} therefore gives
\[
|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)|
\le
F\|b_x-q_x\|_1+d^{1/4}F\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}
\le
2d^{1/4}F\,\Gamma_x(N').
\]
With \(\mu_x^{\mathrm{pil}}=\bigotimes_{\jmath}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_\jmath)\) and
\(\mu_x^{\mathrm{eval}}=\bigotimes_{\jmath}\operatorname{Pois}(m q_\jmath)\), set
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}
=
\int_{\mathsf G_x^c}
|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)|
\,d(\mu_x^{\mathrm{pil}}\otimes\mu_x^{\mathrm{eval}})
\]
and
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}
=
\int_{\mathsf G_x^c}
(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x))^2
\,d(\mu_x^{\mathrm{pil}}\otimes\mu_x^{\mathrm{eval}}).
\]
% lean: canonicalJacksonCellError_le_normalizedAggregateScore
% lean: canonicalNormalizedAggregateScore_memLp_two

\item \emph{Good-pilot scales.}
By \cref{obj:lem:good-pilot-radius-sum}, on \(\mathsf G_x\),
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}
\le
2\cdot10^6
\left\{\sqrt{\frac{p_xL}{m}}+\frac Lm\right\}.
\]
Set
\[
v_x=\sqrt{\frac{p_xL}{m}}+\frac Lm,\qquad
\eta_x=\sqrt{\frac{p_x}{mL}}+\frac1{mL}.
\]
Since \(L\ge1\),
\[
v_x=L\sqrt{\frac{p_x}{mL}}+\frac Lm\le L^2\eta_x.
\]
For \(K_d=\max\{2,\lfloor L/100000\rfloor\}\), first
\[
\frac L{K_d}\le150000,
\qquad
\left(\frac L{K_d}\right)^2\le150000^2 .
\]
Indeed, if \(L\le3\cdot10^5\), then \(K_d\ge2\) gives \(L/K_d\le150000\).  If
\(L>3\cdot10^5\), then
\[
K_d\ge \frac L{100000}-1\ge \frac L{150000},
\]
and the same bound follows.

The logarithmic calibrations used below are
\[
L d^{-3/16}\le7,
\qquad
L d^{1/4}e^{-20L}\le7,
\qquad
d^{1/2}e^{-20L}\le d^{1/16},
\]
and
\[
L^2d^{-3/16}\le225,
\qquad
L^2d^{1/4}e^{-20L}\le225 .
\]
For \(d\ge1\), the calculus inequality \(\log y\le y^a/a\) for \(y\ge1\) gives, with
\(a=3/16\),
\[
L=1+\log d\le d^{3/16}+\frac{16}{3}d^{3/16}\le7d^{3/16},
\]
which proves \(Ld^{-3/16}\le7\).  Since \(L\ge1\),
\[
e^{-20L}\le e^{-L}=\frac1{ed}\le\frac1d .
\]
Consequently
\[
Ld^{1/4}e^{-20L}\le Ld^{-3/4}\le Ld^{-3/16}\le7,
\]
and
\[
d^{1/2}e^{-20L}\le d^{-1/2}\le d^{1/16}.
\]
With \(a=3/32\), the same calculus inequality gives
\[
L=1+\log d\le d^{3/32}+\frac{32}{3}d^{3/32}\le15d^{3/32},
\]
so
\[
L^2\le225d^{3/16}.
\]
Multiplying by \(d^{-3/16}\) proves \(L^2d^{-3/16}\le225\), and using
\(e^{-20L}\le1/d\) once more gives
\[
L^2d^{1/4}e^{-20L}\le L^2d^{-3/4}
\le L^2d^{-3/16}\le225 .
\]
% lean: pilotGoodEvent_mem_pilotRectangle
% lean: canonicalPilotGoodRadiusSum_le
% lean: canonicalLogPowerBounds
% lean: canonicalLogSquaredPowerBounds

\item \label{item:pf-good-pilot-factorial-ltwo}\emph{Good-pilot factorial \(L_2\) bound.}
Fix a pilot table in \(\mathsf G_x\), and write
\[
\chi_{\jmath,x}=\sqrt{\frac{c_{\jmath,x}L}{m}},
\qquad
h_{\jmath,x}=1024\left(\chi_{\jmath,x}+\frac Lm\right).
\]
First,
\[
\frac{q_\jmath}{m r_{\jmath,x}^2}\le\frac1L .
\]
Indeed, the good-pilot inequality gives
\[
q_\jmath\le c_{\jmath,x}+\frac14h_{\jmath,x}
\le 384\left(c_{\jmath,x}+\frac Lm\right),
\]
where the last inequality uses
\(\chi_{\jmath,x}\le (c_{\jmath,x}+L/m)/2\).  Since
\(r_{\jmath,x}\ge h_{\jmath,x}/2=512(\chi_{\jmath,x}+L/m)\),
\[
m r_{\jmath,x}^2
\ge
m\,512^2\left(\chi_{\jmath,x}+\frac Lm\right)^2
\ge
384L\left(c_{\jmath,x}+\frac Lm\right),
\]
because \(\chi_{\jmath,x}^2=c_{\jmath,x}L/m\).  Combining the last two displays proves the ratio bound.

Also \(q_x\in Q_x\) gives
\[
|q_\jmath-b_{\jmath,x}|\le r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
For any nonnegative integer \(h\), the centered-factorial second-moment identity from \cref{item:pf-centered-factorial-moments} yields
\[
E\left[
\left\{
\frac{U_h(N_{\jmath,x};b_{\jmath,x})}{r_{\jmath,x}^h}
\right\}^2
\middle|N'
\right]
\le
\sum_{\ell=0}^{h}\binom h\ell^2\ell!L^{-\ell}
\le
\exp(h^2/L).
\]
Let \(P_{x,K_d}\) be the Jackson tensor polynomial on \(Q_x\), and write the centered normalized expansion from
\cref{obj:lem:jackson-normalized-coefficient-envelope} as
\[
P_{x,K_d}(v)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}b_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left(\frac{v_\jmath-b_{\jmath,x}}{r_{\jmath,x}}\right)^{\alpha_\jmath},
\]
with
\[
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}|b_{x,\alpha}|
\le (2^{60})^{K_d}F\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x},
\qquad
\alpha_\jmath\le2(K_d-1).
\]
Define the factorial lift
\[
\mathcal L_x(N)
=
\sum_{\alpha\in\mathcal A_x}b_{x,\alpha}
\prod_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\frac{U_{\alpha_\jmath}(N_{\jmath,x};b_{\jmath,x})}
{r_{\jmath,x}^{\alpha_\jmath}} .
\]
Independence of the evaluation coordinates and Minkowski's inequality give
\[
E\{\mathcal L_x(N)^2\mid N'\}
\le
(2^{60})^{2K_d}F^2
\left(\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}\right)^2
\exp(16K_d^2/L).
\]
The calibrated coefficient growth bound is
\[
(2^{60})^{2K_d}\exp(16K_d^2/L)
\le e^{1000}d^{1/16}.
\]
Indeed, \(K_d=\max\{2,\lfloor L/100000\rfloor\}\) and the floor bound give
\[
K_d\le 2+\frac{L}{100000}.
\]
Since \(L\ge1\), squaring the last display gives
\[
K_d^2\le L\left(5+\frac{L}{10^{10}}\right),
\qquad
\frac{K_d^2}{L}\le 5+\frac{L}{10^{10}}.
\]
Also \(L=1+\log d\), and hence
\[
120K_d+16\frac{K_d^2}{L}
\le
120\left(2+\frac{L}{100000}\right)
+16\left(5+\frac{L}{10^{10}}\right)
\le
1000+\frac{\log d}{16}.
\]
Because \(2<e\),
\[
(2^{60})^{2K_d}=2^{120K_d}\le e^{120K_d}.
\]
Multiplying by the remaining exponential factor and using the preceding exponent comparison yields
\[
(2^{60})^{2K_d}\exp(16K_d^2/L)
\le
\exp\left(120K_d+16\frac{K_d^2}{L}\right)
\le
\exp\left(1000+\frac{\log d}{16}\right)
=e^{1000}d^{1/16}.
\]
Therefore
\[
E\{\mathcal L_x(N)^2\mid N'\}
\le
e^{1000}d^{1/16}S_x^2 .
\]
% lean: canonicalPilot_noiseToRadius_le
% lean: jacksonCenteredNormalizedPolynomial_coeffL1_le
% lean: chooseSqFactorialSum_le_exp
% lean: canonicalJacksonCoefficientGrowth
% lean: canonicalGoodPilotFactorialLift_sq_le

\item \label{item:pf-good-pilot-bias}\emph{Good-pilot bias.}
For the fixed good pilot, the factorial identities give
\[
E\{\mathcal L_x(N)\mid N'\}
=
P_{x,K_d}(q_x)-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x).
\]
Let
\[
Z_x=\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)+\mathcal L_x(N),
\qquad
T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}
=
\Pi_{[\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)-\varrho_x,\,
       \bar f_\epsilon(b_x)+\varrho_x]}(Z_x).
\]
Hence
\[
E(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}\mid N')-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)
=
\{P_{x,K_d}(q_x)-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}
+
E(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-Z_x\mid N').
\]
For each coordinate,
\[
\sqrt{(q_\jmath-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath)}
\le
10^7\sqrt{\frac{q_\jmath L}{m}} .
\]
To see this, if \(c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}\le0\), then
\(\ell_{\jmath,x}=0\), and the quadratic inequality
\(c_{\jmath,x}\le h_{\jmath,x}\) gives
\(c_{\jmath,x}\le1.1\cdot10^6L/m\) and
\(h_{\jmath,x}\le1.2\cdot10^9L/m\), so
\[
(q_\jmath-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath)
\le q_\jmath(c_{\jmath,x}+h_{\jmath,x})
\le 10^{14}\frac{q_\jmath L}{m}.
\]
If \(c_{\jmath,x}-h_{\jmath,x}>0\), then the good-pilot inequality gives
\(q_\jmath\ge3c_{\jmath,x}/4\) and \(L/m\le c_{\jmath,x}\), whence
\[
h_{\jmath,x}\le4096\sqrt{\frac{q_\jmath L}{m}},
\qquad
\sqrt{(q_\jmath-\ell_{\jmath,x})(u_{\jmath,x}-q_\jmath)}
\le r_{\jmath,x}=h_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Applying \cref{obj:lem:simultaneous-jackson-certificate} at \(q_x\in Q_x\) gives
\[
|P_{x,K_d}(q_x)-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)|
\le
C_J
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left\{
\frac{10^7\sqrt{q_\jmath L/m}}{K_d}
+
\frac{r_{\jmath,x}}{K_d^2}
\right\}.
\]
Since \(\sum_{\jmath}\sqrt{q_\jmath L/m}\le2\sqrt{p_xL/m}\) and
\(\sum_{\jmath}r_{\jmath,x}\le2\cdot10^6v_x\),
\[
\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}
\left\{
\frac{10^7\sqrt{q_\jmath L/m}}{K_d}
+
\frac{r_{\jmath,x}}{K_d^2}
\right\}
\le
22\cdot10^6\frac{\sqrt{p_xL/m}}{K_d}
+
2\cdot10^6\frac{L/m}{K_d^2}
\le
5\cdot10^{16}\eta_x .
\]
From the interval projection definition in \cref{obj:synth_13}, a direct case split gives the following elementary consequence: for every real midpoint \(\operatorname{mid}\), every real input \(\operatorname{raw}\), and every radius \(\operatorname{rad}>0\),
\[
\left|
\Pi_{[\operatorname{mid}-\operatorname{rad},\operatorname{mid}+\operatorname{rad}]}
(\operatorname{raw})-\operatorname{raw}
\right|
\le
\frac{(\operatorname{raw}-\operatorname{mid})^2}{\operatorname{rad}}.
\]
If \(\operatorname{raw}\) lies inside the interval, the left side is zero.  If
\(\operatorname{raw}<\operatorname{mid}-\operatorname{rad}\), then the inequality follows after multiplying by \(\operatorname{rad}\) from
\[
(\operatorname{raw}-\operatorname{mid})^2
+\operatorname{rad}(\operatorname{raw}-\operatorname{mid})
+\operatorname{rad}^2\ge0,
\]
and the case \(\operatorname{raw}>\operatorname{mid}+\operatorname{rad}\) is identical with the middle sign reversed.  Applying this with midpoint \(\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)\), radius \(\varrho_x\), and input \(Z_x\), and using
\(Z_x-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)=\mathcal L_x(N)\), gives
\[
|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-Z_x|
\le
\frac{\mathcal L_x(N)^2}{\varrho_x}.
\]
Using the factorial \(L_2\) bound from \cref{item:pf-good-pilot-factorial-ltwo},
\[
|E(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-Z_x\mid N')|
\le
\frac{e^{1000}d^{1/16}S_x^2}{d^{1/4}S_x}
=
e^{1000}F d^{-3/16}\sum_{\jmath\in\mathcal J}r_{\jmath,x}.
\]
Together with the good-pilot radius bound and \(L^2d^{-3/16}\le225\), this implies
\[
\left|
E(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}\mid N')-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)
\right|
\le
\{5\cdot10^{16}C_J+450000000\,e^{1000}F\}\eta_x
\qquad\text{on }\mathsf G_x .
\]
% lean: canonicalGoodPilotConditionalBias

\item \emph{Good-pilot second moment.}
On \(\mathsf G_x\), the four-coordinate Lipschitz bound from \cref{item:pf-pilot-geometry-failure} and
\(q_x\in Q_x\) give
\[
|\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)|\le S_x .
\]
A direct case split using the interval projection \(\Pi_{[a,b]}\) from \cref{obj:synth_13} gives the contraction
\[
\left|
\Pi_{[\operatorname{mid}-\operatorname{rad},\operatorname{mid}+\operatorname{rad}]}
(\operatorname{raw})-\operatorname{mid}
\right|
\le
|\operatorname{raw}-\operatorname{mid}|.
\]
Applying it with midpoint \(\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)\), radius \(\varrho_x\), and input \(Z_x\) gives
\[
|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(b_x)|
\le |\mathcal L_x(N)|.
\]
Therefore
\[
|T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)|
\le |\mathcal L_x(N)|+S_x,
\]
and hence
\[
(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x))^2
\le
2\mathcal L_x(N)^2+2S_x^2.
\]
After conditional expectation over the evaluation counts,
\[
E\!\left[
\{T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}^2
\mid N'
\right]
\le
2e^{1000}d^{1/16}S_x^2+2S_x^2
\le
4e^{1000}d^{1/16}S_x^2,
\]
because \(1\le e^{1000}d^{1/16}\).  Integrating over pilots on \(\mathsf G_x\) and using
\(\sum_{\jmath}r_{\jmath,x}\le2\cdot10^6v_x\) gives
\[
E\!\left[
\{T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}^2\mathbf 1_{\mathsf G_x}
\right]
\le
4e^{1000}d^{1/16}(2\cdot10^6Fv_x)^2 .
\]
% lean: canonicalGoodPilotConditionalMSE

\item \emph{Bad-pilot bounds.}
The pointwise domination from \cref{item:pf-pilot-geometry-failure} and \cref{obj:lem:canonical-pilot-bad-moment} give
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}
\le
2d^{1/4}FC_1e^{-20L}v_x
\]
and
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}
\le
(2d^{1/4}F)^2C_2e^{-20L}v_x^2 .
\]
Using \(v_x\le L^2\eta_x\) and \(L^2d^{1/4}e^{-20L}\le225\),
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}\le450FC_1\,\eta_x .
\]
Also
\[
v_x^2
=
\left(\sqrt{\frac{p_xL}{m}}+\frac Lm\right)^2
\le
2\left\{\frac{p_xL}{m}+\frac{L^2}{m^2}\right\},
\]
and \(d^{1/2}e^{-20L}\le d^{1/16}\), so
\[
\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}
\le
8F^2C_2d^{1/16}
\left\{\frac{p_xL}{m}+\frac{L^2}{m^2}\right\}.
\]
The displayed estimates are exactly the asserted pilot-failure bounds after enlarging by
\(C_\epsilon\).
% lean: canonicalPilotFailureBounds

\item \emph{Bias.}
Disintegrating over the pilot table, the good-pilot conditional bound from \cref{item:pf-good-pilot-bias} and the bad-pilot contribution give
\[
\left|
E T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)
\right|
\le
\{5\cdot10^{16}C_J+450000000\,e^{1000}F\}\eta_x+\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}.
\]
Using the estimate for \(\mathrm{PF}_{1,x}\) and the definition of \(C_\epsilon\),
\[
\left|
E T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)
\right|
\le
C_\epsilon
\left\{
\sqrt{\frac{p_x}{mL_d}}+\frac1{mL_d}
\right\}.
\]
% lean: centered_factorial_pilot_control

\item \emph{Variance.}
Combining the good-pilot second-moment estimate with \(\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}\) yields
\[
E\{T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}^2
\le
4e^{1000}d^{1/16}(2\cdot10^6Fv_x)^2+\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}.
\]
Using the bound on \(v_x^2\), the estimate for \(\mathrm{PF}_{2,x}\), and the definition of
\(C_\epsilon\),
\[
E\{T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}^2
\le
C_\epsilon d^{1/16}
\left\{
\frac{p_xL_d}{m}+\frac{L_d^2}{m^2}
\right\}.
\]
Variance is bounded by the mean squared deviation about any fixed center, so
\[
\operatorname{Var}(T_x^{\mathrm{JF}})
\le
E\{T_x^{\mathrm{JF}}-\bar f_\epsilon(q_x)\}^2.
\]
Thus the stated bias, variance, and pilot-failure estimates hold uniformly in the cell \(x\), in
\(n,d\), and in \(\mathbb P\in\mathcal D_{d,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{obs}}\).
% lean: cellStatisticVariance
% lean: centered_factorial_pilot_control
\end{enumerate}
\end{proof}

\Cref{obj:lem:centered-factorial-pilot-control} supplies the cellwise estimates used to prove the upper bound. The first displayed identities identify the Poissonized marked experiment with independent pilot and evaluation arrays. The factorial identities then center the polynomial at the pilot rectangle center, and the final bounds control bias, variance, and pilot-failure terms at the same local scale.


\section{Moment matching and lower-bound details}

This appendix gives the dense lower-bound construction used in \cref{obj:thm:all-estimator-lower}. The argument works inside an equal-mass, equal-propensity submodel and converts best polynomial approximation of \(|t|\) into a separation in the optimal-value target. Its testing step follows the classical moment-matching method for nonsmooth functionals \citep{CaiLow2011L1}, with Poissonized mixtures used to make the likelihood calculation cellwise.

We first state the Poissonized risk that appears in the comparison. It is the same minimax squared loss criterion as \cref{obj:def:minimax-risk}, evaluated under a Poisson observed-sample law and with the estimator projected to the target range.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_16}[Poisson minimax risk]
For \(m,d\in\mathbb N\) and \(\epsilon\in\mathbb R\), define the Poissonized minimax squared risk
\[
\mathfrak R^{\mathrm{Pois}}_{m,d,\epsilon}
:=
\inf_{\widehat V}
\sup_{\mathbb P\in\mathcal D_{d,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{obs}}}
\mathbb E_{\operatorname{Pois}(m,\mathbb P)}
\left[
\left\{
\Pi_{[0,1]}\bigl(\widehat V(\mathcal S)\bigr)
-\Psi(\mathbb P)
\right\}^{2}
\right].
\]
Here \(\mathcal S\) is the finite observed sample drawn from the Poisson observed-sample law with mean \(m\) and atom law \(\mathbb P\), the supremum ranges over the observed-law model class in \(\cref{obj:def:observed-model-class}\), the target \(\Psi(\mathbb P)\) is the observed optimal value from \(\cref{obj:def:observed-optimal-value}\), and the infimum ranges over all dense Poisson estimators on the \(d\)-cell observed sample space.
\end{definitionv}

The submodel is indexed by a dense contrast vector. Each cell has mass \(1/d\), treatment probability \(1/2\), and opposite perturbations of the two arm-specific outcome means.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_15}[Dense contrast law]
For an integer \(d\geq 2\) and a contrast vector \(\theta=(\theta_x)_{x\in[d]}\) with \(\theta_x\in[-1/2,1/2]\) for every \(x\in[d]\), define \(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}}\) as the full-data probability law on \((X,A,Y,Y(0),Y(1))\) whose atom at \((x,a,y,y_0,y_1)\in[d]\times\{0,1\}^4\) is
\[
P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}}(x,a,y,y_0,y_1)
=
\begin{cases}
\dfrac{1}{2d}\, b_{(1-\theta_x)/2}(y_0)\, b_{(1+\theta_x)/2}(y_1),
& y=y_a,\\[0.8em]
0,
& y\neq y_a,
\end{cases}
\]
where \(y_a=y_1\) if \(a=1\) and \(y_a=y_0\) if \(a=0\), and \(b_\mu(1)=\mu\), \(b_\mu(0)=1-\mu\).
\end{definitionv}

For this law, the target in \cref{obj:def:observed-optimal-value} becomes an average of \(|\theta_x|\) plus the common baseline. The lower bound therefore reduces estimation of the value to estimation of an absolute-value functional over many small coordinates.

The construction below fixes the moment-matching degree, scales the contrast amplitude, and defines the two product priors. The existence of the symmetric measures \(\nu_{0,K}\) and \(\nu_{1,K}\) with matching moments through degree \(K\) and separation \(2E_K\) is the approximation-theoretic input from \citet[Lemma 1 and Section 3.1]{CaiLow2011L1}; the same source gives constants \(0<c<C<\infty\) with \(c/K\le E_K\le C/K\).

\begin{definitionv}{def:dense-moment-matching-construction}[Dense moment-matching construction]
For \(n,d\in\mathbb N\), \(\epsilon\in\mathbb R\), the overlap parameter, a dense domain certificate
\[
2\le d,\qquad
0<\epsilon,\qquad
\epsilon<\frac12,\qquad
0<a_{n,d}\le \frac12,
\]
and a dense prior family \(K\mapsto(\nu_{0,K},\nu_{1,K})\) indexed by positive even degrees, define the dense moment-matching construction as follows.  For every positive even \(K\), the family carries the prior-pair conditions that \(\nu_{0,K}\) and \(\nu_{1,K}\) are probability measures supported on \([-1,1]\), are symmetric, have matching moments through degree \(K\), and satisfy
\[
\int_{-1}^1 |t|\,d\nu_{1,K}(t)
-
\int_{-1}^1 |t|\,d\nu_{0,K}(t)
=
2E_K .
\]
The construction has components:
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Dense law.)} For each \(\theta\in[-1/2,1/2]^d\), \(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}}\) is the potential-outcome law induced by the dense full-data masses, equivalently \(p_x=1/d\), \(\pi_x=1/2\), \(\mu_{1x}=(1+\theta_x)/2\), and \(\mu_{0x}=(1-\theta_x)/2\), with \(Y(0)\) and \(Y(1)\) conditionally independent Bernoulli variables given \(X=x\), \(A\mid X=x\sim\operatorname{Bernoulli}(1/2)\) independently of the potential outcomes, and \(Y=Y(A)\).
\item \textbf{(Approximation scale.)} The approximation degree and best even-degree approximation error are
\[
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}=2\lceil4L_d\rceil,
\qquad
E_K=\inf\left\{e\in\mathbb R:\exists p,\ \deg(p)\le K,\ 
\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}\bigl||t|-p(t)\bigr|\le e\right\}.
\]
The construction uses \(E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}\).
\item \textbf{(Poisson intensity and amplitude.)} The one-cell Poisson intensity and dense contrast amplitude are
\[
\lambda_{n,d}=\frac{2n}{d},
\qquad
a_{n,d}=\sqrt{\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}}.
\]
\item \textbf{(Coordinate priors.)} The coordinate priors are
\[
\nu_{0,K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}},
\qquad
\nu_{1,K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}.
\]
\item \textbf{(Dense product priors.)} The priors \(\Pi_{0,n,d}\) and \(\Pi_{1,n,d}\) are the pushforwards into the dense contrast class of the restrictions of \(\nu_{0,K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}^{\otimes d}\) and \(\nu_{1,K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}^{\otimes d}\) to \([-1,1]^d\), under coordinatewise scaling by \(a_{n,d}\).
\item \textbf{(Observation mixtures.)} The mixtures \(\mathbb M_{0,n,d}\) and \(\mathbb M_{1,n,d}\) are the observation-kernel mixtures induced by \(\Pi_{0,n,d}\) and \(\Pi_{1,n,d}\), with independent Poissonized sample size \(\operatorname{Pois}(2n)\).
\item \textbf{(Risk and separation.)} The risk component is the Poissonized minimax risk
\[
\mathfrak R^{\mathrm{Pois}}_{2n,d,\epsilon}.
\]
The prior target-mean separation is
\[
\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}
=
E_{\Pi_{1,n,d}}\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
-
E_{\Pi_{0,n,d}}\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}.
\]
\end{itemize}
The construction carries the supplied prior-pair certificate at degree \(K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\) and the identities
\[
K=K_d^{\mathrm{lb}},
\qquad
\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}
=
a_{n,d}E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}.
\]
\end{definitionv}

The amplitude condition keeps the constructed contrasts inside the dense law in \cref{obj:synth_15}. In the range \(d^2<n\), the logarithmic degree is small enough relative to the one-cell Poisson intensity to ensure this admissibility.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:dense-amplitude-range}[Dense amplitude range]
Let \(n,d\in\mathbb N\) satisfy \(2\le d\) and \(d^2<n\). For the lower-bound contrast amplitude \(a_{n,d}\) defined in \cref{obj:def:dense-moment-matching-construction},
\[
0<a_{n,d}\le \frac12.
\]
\end{lemmav}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:dense-amplitude-range}]
Write
\[
L_d=\log(ed),\qquad
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}=2\lceil 4L_d\rceil,\qquad
\lambda_{n,d}=\frac{2n}{d},
\qquad
a_{n,d}=\sqrt{\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}},
\]
as in \cref{obj:def:dense-moment-matching-construction}.  Since \(2\le d\), we have \(0<d\) and hence, as real numbers,
\[
\log d\le d-1.
\]
Moreover
\[
L_d=\log(ed)=1+\log d,
\]
so
\[
L_d\le d.
\]
Also \(ed>1\), and therefore
\[
0<L_d.
\]
It follows that
\[
0<\lceil 4L_d\rceil,
\qquad
0<K_d^{\mathrm{lb}},
\]
and, using \(L_d\le d\),
\[
\lceil 4L_d\rceil\le 4d,
\qquad
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}=2\lceil 4L_d\rceil\le 8d .
\]
% lean: denseAmplitude_range

The assumption \(d^2<n\) gives \(0<n\), so
\[
0<\lambda_{n,d}=\frac{2n}{d}.
\]
Consequently
\[
0<
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}},
\]
and hence
\[
0<a_{n,d}.
\]
% lean: denseAmplitude_range

It remains to bound the square-root argument by \(1/4\).  Since
\[
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\le 8d
\]
and \(d^2<n\), we have
\[
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}d\le 8d^2<8n\le 32n .
\]
Dividing by the positive quantity \(32n\) gives
\[
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}d}{32n}\le 1.
\]
Equivalently, because \(\lambda_{n,d}=2n/d\),
\[
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}
=
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}d}{128n}
\le
\frac14
=
\left(\frac12\right)^2 .
\]
Taking square roots yields
\[
a_{n,d}
=
\sqrt{\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}}
\le
\frac12 .
\]
Combining this with \(0<a_{n,d}\) proves the claim.
% lean: denseAmplitude_range
\end{proof}

The testing comparison is expressed in total variation. The next definitions isolate the distance, the likelihood tail left after moment matching, and the one-cell likelihood ratio used in the product calculation.

\begin{definitionv}{synth_14}[Total variation distance]
For probability laws \(\mathbb M_0\) and \(\mathbb M_1\) on a common measurable space, define \(\operatorname{TV}(\mathbb M_0,\mathbb M_1)=\sup_{B}\lvert \mathbb M_0(B)-\mathbb M_1(B)\rvert\), where the supremum ranges over measurable events \(B\).
\end{definitionv}

\begin{definitionv}{synth_11}[Dense likelihood tail]
For the dense lower-bound degree \(K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\), Poissonized cell intensity \(\lambda_{n,d}=2n/d\), and dense amplitude \(a_{n,d}\), define the dense likelihood tail by \[\operatorname{tail}_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}:=\sum_{r=K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}+1}^{\infty}\frac{(\lambda_{n,d}a_{n,d}^2)^r}{r!}.\]
\end{definitionv}

\begin{definitionv}{synth_9}[One-cell dense likelihood ratio]
Fix a Poisson cell intensity \(\lambda\ge0\). Let \(R_+\) and \(R_-\) be the two sign counts in one dense cell, and let \(E_0\) denote expectation under the zero-contrast baseline law \(R_+,R_-\stackrel{\mathrm{ind}}{\sim}\operatorname{Pois}(\lambda/2)\). For \(\theta\in[-1,1]\), the one-cell likelihood ratio relative to this baseline is \[ L_\theta(R_+,R_-):=(1+\theta)^{R_+}(1-\theta)^{R_-}. \] In the dense lower bound, \(L_{\theta_x}\) applies this definition with the cell contrast \(\theta_x\) and \(\lambda=\lambda_{n,d}\).
\end{definitionv}

Moment matching removes the first \(K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\) likelihood terms, so the remaining total-variation control is governed by the tail in \cref{obj:synth_11}. The lower bound then applies the standard fuzzy-hypothesis testing reduction of \citet{LeCam1986} and \citet{Tsybakov2009}: two priors with separated target means, concentrated targets, and small induced total variation force squared risk at the square of the separation.

\begin{lemmav}{lem:dense-moment-matching-lower}[Dense moment matching lower bound]*
Suppose the product experiment satisfies the i.i.d. sampling law in \cref{obj:ass:iid-sampling}.  There is an integer cutoff \(D_0\geq2\) such that, for every overlap constant \(\epsilon\in(0,1/2)\), there is a constant \(c_\epsilon>0\) with the following property.  For all integers \(d,n\) satisfying \(d\geq D_0\) and \(d^2<n\):
\begin{itemize}
\item \textbf{(Degree and amplitude.)} The lower-bound degree and amplitude obey
\[
8L_d\leq K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}<8L_d+2,
\qquad
a_{n,d}^2=\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}d}{128n},
\qquad
a_{n,d}\leq \frac12 .
\]
\item \textbf{(Dense observed submodel.)} For every \(\theta\in[-a_{n,d},a_{n,d}]^d\), the dense equal-mass contrast submodel \(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}}\) has observed margin in \(\mathcal D_{d,\epsilon}^{\mathrm{obs}}\) and satisfies, for every \(x\in[d]\),
\[
p_x=\frac1d,
\qquad
\pi_x=\frac12,
\qquad
\mu_{1x}=\frac{1+\theta_x}{2},
\qquad
\mu_{0x}=\frac{1-\theta_x}{2},
\]
with
\[
\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
=
\frac12+\frac{1}{2d}\sum_{x=1}^d|\theta_x|.
\]
\item \textbf{(Moment-matched priors and mixtures.)} The two dense product priors \(\Pi_{0,n,d}\) and \(\Pi_{1,n,d}\), with induced Poissonized mixtures \(\mathbb M_{0,n,d}\) and \(\mathbb M_{1,n,d}\), may be chosen so that their prior-mean separation satisfies
\[
\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}
=
a_{n,d}E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}},
\qquad
0<\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}\leq1,
\]
and
\[
\operatorname{TV}(\mathbb M_{0,n,d},\mathbb M_{1,n,d})
\leq
d\{\operatorname{tail}_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}\}^{1/2}
\leq
d\left\{\frac{(e/64)^{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}+1}}{1-e/64}\right\}^{1/2}
\leq\frac1{16}.
\]
Under each of the two product priors, the target is concentrated around its prior mean:
\[
\int
\left(\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
-\mathbb E_{\Pi_{j,n,d}}\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}\right)^2
\,d\Pi_{j,n,d}(\theta)
\leq
\frac{a_{n,d}^2}{4d},
\qquad j\in\{0,1\},
\]
and
\[
\Pi_{j,n,d}\left(
\left|\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
-\mathbb E_{\Pi_{j,n,d}}\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}\right|
>
\frac{\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}}{4}
\right)
\leq
\frac18,
\qquad j\in\{0,1\}.
\]
\item \textbf{(One-cell likelihood identity.)} For all contrasts \(\theta,\theta'\in\mathbb R\),
\[
E_0[L_\theta L_{\theta'}]
=
\exp(\lambda_{n,d}\theta\theta').
\]
\end{itemize}
Consequently,
\[
c_\epsilon(\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}})^2
\leq
\mathfrak R^{\mathrm{Pois}}_{2n,d,\epsilon},
\qquad
c_\epsilon\frac{d}{nK_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}
\leq
\mathfrak R^{\mathrm{Pois}}_{2n,d,\epsilon},
\]
and the fixed-sample and Poissonized risks satisfy
\[
\mathfrak R_{n,d,\epsilon}
\geq
\mathfrak R^{\mathrm{Pois}}_{2n,d,\epsilon}
-
\Pr\{\operatorname{Pois}(2n)<n\}.
\]
In particular,
\[
c_\epsilon\frac{d}{nL_d}
\leq
\mathfrak R_{n,d,\epsilon}.
\]
\end{lemmav}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-BEGIN lem:dense-moment-matching-lower
\verificationfootnotetext{\textbf{Formalization scope.} This result uses the published conclusion \citep{CaiLow2011L1} (Lemma 1 and Section 3.1). The cited source proof is not formalized here: Lean verifies its use and all remaining steps. If that cited conclusion is false, the portions of this result that depend on it are not certified.}
% CAUSALSMITH-CITED-SCOPE-END lem:dense-moment-matching-lower

\begin{proof}[Proof of \cref{obj:lem:dense-moment-matching-lower}]
Let
\[
L_d=\log(ed),\qquad
K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}=2\lceil 4L_d\rceil,\qquad
\lambda_{n,d}=\frac{2n}{d},\qquad
a_{n,d}=\sqrt{\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}}.
\]
By \cref{obj:lem:dense-amplitude-range}, the standing conditions \(2\le d\) and \(d^2<n\) give
\[
0<a_{n,d}\le \frac12.
\]
The ceiling bounds also give
\[
8L_d\le K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}<8L_d+2.
\]
If \(d\ge2\), then \(L_d=1+\log d\le d\). Hence
\[
4L_d\le 4d,\qquad \lceil 4L_d\rceil\le 4d,
\]
because \(4d\) is an integer above \(4L_d\). Therefore \(K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\le8d\). If also \(d^2<n\), then \(\lambda_{n,d}>0\), and
\[
a_{n,d}^2
=
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}{64\lambda_{n,d}}
=
\frac{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}d}{128n}.
\]
These relations, together with the amplitude range supplied above, prove the first group of claims.

For \(\theta\in[-a_{n,d},a_{n,d}]^d\), the amplitude bound places every coordinate in \([-1/2,1/2]\). The dense law of \cref{obj:def:dense-moment-matching-construction} has, in every cell,
\[
p_x=\frac1d,\qquad
\pi_x=\frac12,\qquad
\mu_{1x}=\frac{1+\theta_x}{2},\qquad
\mu_{0x}=\frac{1-\theta_x}{2},
\]
equivalently
\[
q_{11,x}=q_{00,x}=\frac{1+\theta_x}{4d},
\qquad
q_{10,x}=q_{01,x}=\frac{1-\theta_x}{4d}.
\]
Since \(0<\epsilon<1/2\), the propensity \(1/2\) belongs to \([\epsilon,1-\epsilon]\), and the displayed means belong to \([0,1]\). Thus the observed margin lies in the class of \cref{obj:def:observed-model-class}. By \cref{obj:def:observed-optimal-value},
\[
\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
=
\frac1d\sum_{x=1}^d
\max\left\{\frac{1+\theta_x}{2},\frac{1-\theta_x}{2}\right\}
=
\frac12+\frac1{2d}\sum_{x=1}^d|\theta_x|.
\]
By \citep[Lemma 1 and Section 3.1]{CaiLow2011L1}, for every positive even \(K\) there are symmetric probability measures \(\nu_{0,K}\) and \(\nu_{1,K}\) on \([-1,1]\), with matching moments through degree \(K\), such that
\[
\int |t|\,d\nu_{1,K}(t)-\int |t|\,d\nu_{0,K}(t)=2E_K,
\qquad
\frac{c_{\mathrm{CL}}}{K}\le E_K\le \frac{C_{\mathrm{CL}}}{K}
\]
for universal constants \(0<c_{\mathrm{CL}}<C_{\mathrm{CL}}<\infty\). Choose these priors at \(K=K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\), take their \(d\)-fold products, and map each coordinate by \(t\mapsto a_{n,d}t\). The preceding display for \(\Psi\) gives
\[
\mathbb E_{\Pi_{j,n,d}}\Psi\{\operatorname{Obs}(P_\theta^{\mathrm{dense}})\}
=
\frac12+\frac{a_{n,d}}{2d}
\sum_{x=1}^d\int |t|\,d\nu_{j,K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}(t),
\]
and hence
\[
\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}
=
a_{n,d}E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}.
\]
The lower Cai--Low bound gives \(E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}>0\). The zero polynomial is an admissible degree-\(K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}\) approximant to \(|t|\), and
\[
\sup_{t\in[-1,1]}||t|-0|\le1,
\]
so \(E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}\le1\). Together with \(0<a_{n,d}\le1/2\), this yields
\[
0<\Delta_{n,d}^{\mathrm{dense}}\le1.
\]
The same reciprocal approximation bound supplies a universal cutoff \(D_{\mathrm{scale}}\) such that, for every \(d\ge D_{\mathrm{scale}}\),
\[
32\le d\,E_{K_d^{\mathrm{lb}}}^{2}.
\]
